ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3310	We're coming to the 2nd. Question No 30, by Mr Pendl: Please, Mr Pendl.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3325	Mrs. Vice chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XXI/M/M_00093/index.shtml -- 93/M]] When to Become You propose an amendment to § 13c of the Wage Act, so that all officials, not only executive officials, are reduced in the event of a longer infirmary?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3353	Please have a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3368	Mr Pendl! The aim of the 2002 Pension Reform Act – I have already explained this several times here in this House – was to create the same conditions for creating the same rules of play, both in civil servants' service law and in the field of private employees, i.e. in the unprotected areas, in terms of remunerated pay in the case of sickness. The fact is that there is a much higher period of illness in the public service compared to the private sector. An official has an average of 14.4 days of absenteeism, but an employee only 11.4 days. So you see that there is a certain discrepancy here. It was necessary to make a regulation here, since the relevant provisions do not prevent abuse in accordance with the current legislation. The fact is that, in the case of the execution of § 13c, as it was included in the last version of the Pension Act, special hardships have occurred in a very specific case, namely in the field of executive officials, because, as you know of course, the basic salary in this group is low and the total earnings are composed of allowances and additional fees. In this respect, it was right to make a regulation here, which does not lead to unjustified or over-dimensional reductions in reference. I therefore intend to propose a change in the Committee's deliberations in this House, particularly in those areas which have suffered from the loss of extra costs. This is the case, as has been said, especially in the field of executives. Under the new scheme, the official will continue to pay 80 percent of the sum of his current total income, i.e. the monthly subscription, including the accompanying fees, remunerations, payments and so on. If the monthly reference amounts to less than 80 percent, there is no reduction anyway. The purpose of this more favourable scheme is to replace the previous scheme, thus also having a positive effect on cases where reference reductions have already been made.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3401	Would you like an additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3417	Mrs. Vice chancellor! We're talking about six months and not 14 days. It ensures that there is no undue hardness. On the other hand, it also ensures that there is justice between professional groups.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3495	Mr President. Dear Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! What substantial improvements in service and remuneration law are you planning on the part of your department for the next time, in addition to the already mentioned and upcoming amendment of § 13c of the salary law?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3511	Please, Mrs. Vice chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e3526	Mr deputies, there are in the field of... [[Intrusion of the Abg. You son of a bitch.]] It's nice that you're curious, I'll be happy to satisfy your curiosity, my colleague. [[Abg. Dr. Mertel: You do not satisfy curiosity, you fulfil a duty!]] There will be a whole series of measures in the field of service law novelle in the summer, which will lead to an improvement. For example, provision is made for a scheme relating to leave, which ensures that periods of temporary leave in an international organisation are fully taken into account. This is necessary because, for Austria, it is particularly important to be represented by appropriate qualified staff in international organisations, including within the European Union, without causing service disadvantages. We have seen in the past that Austria has had a major deficit on this point, because many public service employees have not accepted such positions on the basis of the complicated accounting rules that were not satisfactory. In this respect, therefore, a scheme is planned to prepare this situation. In addition, we have foreseen – and I have already reported this here in this House – that the Guards’ Aid Act will be amended. Due to the tragic accident on the south-east of Vienna, in which two policemen were killed, it became apparent that the current legal situation is so restrictive and narrow that it was not possible to grant assistance to the survivors under this title of law. However, I have done so, have given an appropriate direction, but at the same time, by means of this draft, I have also ensured that, in the future, the legal situation is so clear that these cases can be regulated and paid out accordingly. We have also provided for an advance of pain money. This is particularly important for executive officials who have been granted pain money in court on the basis of injuries committed in the service, but in some cases this pain allowance proves to be difficult or not presentable at all. In these cases, then, an advance is useful in order not to impose an unnecessary period of procedure on officials at their own expense. Thirdly, we have provided that – and this was also a gap in the legal situation so far – the time spent by an executive in court or in an administrative authority in the event of an acquittal or a termination of proceedings as Service time is valued and is to be paid in proportion as leisure time.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e4145	I would ask for a reply, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e4160	Mrs Haller! I should also like to point out that last week in Graz, with family politicians and family politicians from all countries, the relevant speakers and their employees, there was a good consensus that, despite the introduction of the childcare allowance, the current family allowance of the countries is fully made available to the families – but in a new form – by the countries. I therefore believe that when introducing the childcare allowance, families with children in their infancy or families with older children should not be afraid that they will come under the wheels and that measures will not be taken comprehensively for them. We have made sure that the new childcare allowance not only improves families with children in the age group from one to three years old, but also sets a corresponding value for families with older children. In addition, I should like to inform you that, just yesterday in Parliament for parents with disabled children, the right to receive the care allowance has been laid down by law before the old age limit, including at the age of zero to three years, so that there will now be a care allowance for disabled children no longer only in individual countries, but in general. I also regard this as an important additional measure of family policy to support the environment of these families and encourage them not only to devote themselves to disabled children but also to meet them with more joy. Care is facilitated at a level of pecuniary, which is of great importance in the humanitarian field, in order to meet families with disabled children.

At the Stockholm European Council a few weeks ago, this was a sustainable and very important issue. Not only is Austria affected, but the whole of European society is affected by two trends: We live longer – that is good – but we also have fewer children – and this is bad, because this creates a demographic gap that becomes a real problem. We only need to look at the number of births: In 1992 – that is not so long ago – we had a total of over 95,000 births. Less than ten years ago we had over 95,000 births, now we are at a low level with 78,000 births! This is not only a material problem for generation solidarity, but it is also a problem of the degree of warmth of a society. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Posch.]] A society with fewer children is a less colorful society, a less joyful society and also a less inclusive society, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Our entire social system lives on solidarity and on the continuity of generations. No pension scheme based on a payment procedure can maintain its stability if the generation of pensioners is to be borne by a too small generation of workers. Nor will a health system – as good as the Austrian – maintain its high standard if that age group, which has a very high medical effort, does not face a generation that compensates for this by its solidarity. [[Abg. Sophie Bauer: But this generation also paid for it before!]] In other words: If the demography comes out of balance, then the generation contract and the generation solidarity are in danger. Nowhere will the solidarity of the generations be better lived and better experienced than in the family, especially if there is also the generation of grandparents, parents and children or grandchildren. Therefore, the family is the central place where this solidarity is experienced, lived and tested. We are concerned about this solidarity through a considerably improved pension system. What we can be proud of is that for the first time in the history of the Austrian pension insurance system, we are assessing child rearing – 18 months at the moment – as a pension-based time. That's a quality jump! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] You know you need at least 180 months of contributions today. In the past, child rearing times were not included because they were only replacement times. In the beginning – and this is just too revolutionary; there is no other country in the world – this time is counted as a pension-based one. I think it's great that Austria is setting an example of how to deal with child, family and pension solidarity worldwide. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The entire family and children strategy is embedded in a very comprehensive social policy concept: We are not only investing – and this despite the toughest sparing pressure – 9 billion schilling more for the child money and for the increase of child support, but we are also investing 7 billion schilling in the education and training of our youth – and I think this is very important – plus 7 billion schilling in research! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Every seventh budget shilling will be spent on the education of young people in Austria this year and next year. This is more than in most other European countries and other industrialised countries. We can be proud of this together: 110 billion Schilling Quality Investment! [[Further congratulation with the ÖVP and the liberals.]] We know that in this area we have to improve a lot earlier and pick up new technologies. Despite the decline in births, we now have for the first time in absolute numbers more apprentices. Through an IT offensive, we increase the quality of training among young people enormously. The number of university graduates who have an IT degree will rise from 800 to 4 200 within two years, thanks to the Minister of Education. The number of graduates with IT qualifications will rise from 20 000 to 30 000 over the next two years. I think this is very essential so that we can really achieve our goal of staying at the forefront of economics, but also of family and social policy. Ladies and gentlemen! I am particularly pleased that a cross-party consensus for the school partnership and for the behavioural agreements was obviously reached in the party discussions last night. I would also like to tell you quite openly why: We must face reality honestly, openly and self-critically. Austria is no longer an island of the Blessed. At a recent Paris conference on the increasing violence in schools, 250 student representatives, teachers and parents took part. ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e5600	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Family policy is the most important policy area for the cohesion of a society. There are surveys of the ranking of values, of what people feel is important in their lives. After that, the family reaches 90 percent and is considered far more important than work, friends, religion or politics, and the trend in this area is increasing. Wherever they are asked, in particular young people with a full life also combine partnership, children and family, but they do not want to be determined on certain roles and do not build up other perspectives from the beginning. Of course, there is also a discrepancy between ideal and reality. However, the consequence cannot be to give up the ideal, but must be to make the framework for reality as good as possible. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Children as a risk of poverty have long become a political keyword. In particular, several families of children, as well as single and single teachers, know that this is much more than a keyword, that is, all too often bitter reality. For too long, family policy has not had that importance on the political agenda that you should and should actually have. Family policy, however, is not a matter of ideological dogmas, but one of life's realities. Families are subject to great strains, and they have to live with many limitations. One only needs to look at the practical everyday life of a family in order to see the many challenges: the working world, the living conditions, the living space for children outside the apartment, the teaching offer, the teaching hours, the opening hours of childcare facilities, the transport connections, educational services, advice and mutual assistance via appropriate networks and facilities. Family policy is one of the very large cross-sectional scenarios in the new century. We have therefore put the focus on family policy, not only in Sunday's speeches, but also in concrete actions that will lead to clear improvements and tangible discharges for all Austrian families. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The focus of our policy is the introduction of child money. This child benefit is a measure that has been available in a federal state in Austria, in Carinthia, since 1.1.2001. From 1.1.2002 on, there will be this child allowance for all of Austria, for all parents, for all mothers and for all fathers. Child benefit is a pure family benefit, no longer an insurance benefit – that has already been addressed – and it is financed exclusively by the Family Tax Compensation Fund. I was very surprised, therefore, when the former Minister of Finance and the current Member of Parliament, Edlinger, had just taken the child's money as a alleged proof that the budget targets would not be met. On the contrary, Mr Edlinger. The difference between you and us is that we also use the money that is intended for the families exclusively and only for this and no longer for stopping budget holes! [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Edlinger: We'll see everything!]] The child's money allows for the freedom of choice for the individual design of family life. It generally recognises the care work or finances the desired form of childcare. This is an advantage especially for low-income families and single parents. For the first time, the freedom of choice is also realized by restrictions of merit, much higher limits of merit than has previously been the case in the case of karenz money. Nor do I understand the criticism in this regard of the socialist opposition at all, because the limits of merit, so to speak, under your aegis, were not even a quarter of what women who receive the child's money can now earn. In this respect, this is an essential and decisive progress not only towards freedom of choice, but also to the compatibility of work and family. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: What the woman Prammer never did!]] It should also not be mentioned that, from the age of 4, children will receive an increase in the allowance by 100 S in order to also set up an initiative towards older children. The unemployment benefit can be obtained for the first time in parallel or following the child care allowance – this is also a very important progress towards the previous situation with the care allowance. It is also necessary to ensure that countries can make qualitative and quantitative improvements in kindergartens through the resources now available at national level in this context. To this end, the allowance for children is a milestone for all mothers both in the private sector and in the public service, but especially for those who have not yet had the opportunity to receive care. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The compatibility of family work and work is at the heart of our family policy. We want mothers and fathers to be able to decide on their own responsibility, in different family phases, to take care of family income and the education of children. Both tasks – family work and work – are equivalent to us and are recognised by us in the same way. This is important because, in the past, we often had to experience that government policy has been oriented to the spirit of time and has created guiding principles in which family work has often been classified as second-class. We want to recognise these extraordinary achievements. Family work is not interchangeable and not announceable! [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Family work is also not a job with winning career prospects. There are no guaranteed occupational protection, holiday or rest periods, and it is not about the intoxicating feeling of an imagery activity in education. But families with children are the basis for a long-term stable economic and social development of our society. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] In the family we learn social virtues and competences. In the family we learn what it means to argue and to put up with ourselves again, to do something alone for ourselves or with others together. The family is still the most viable social network in life's interplays, and these changes will increase in the future. Families do outstanding things in many ways, including in the care work. That is why it was important for us to recognise these achievements in particular. I think it is a very crucial step forward that is now ensured and that the Minister of Social Affairs has ensured that the care cover is extended to include the possibility of payment of care money from the birth of children in the future and not the same as before from the third year of life. The former differentiation that existed here was unfair, and it was not comprehensible. That is why we are very pleased that the Minister for Social Affairs has succeeded in changing this as well. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The integration of disabled people also has a lot to do with family policy. In contrast to previous times, when we were not afraid of reducing the number of disabled people in the budget, we have made sure that the billions of disabled people are available for vocational integration and the improvement of the professional integration of disabled people, i.e. one billion schilling per year. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Our family policy initiative goes across all ministries. It also has to go across all the local authorities: the countries, for example, in the area of childcare facilities, ensuring and ensuring family-friendly and professional opening hours of childcare facilities and, of course, the municipalities, as these are the living conditions for families, housing, transport policy and the like. However, family policy cannot be stopped before the social partners. Employers' and employees' representatives, works councils and trade unions have so far not really seen families at the centre of their tasks or political responsibilities. Of course, some people wonder: Why should entrepreneurs or staff representatives work in their businesses for the families? – The answer to this is actually quite simple: because the economy, too, because the individual business needs the social benefits of the family for a stable community. Successful economy is only possible in stable societies. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The influence of family personality development is therefore also a promotion of employee personality. I therefore think it is particularly important that there are incentives, such as awards for family-friendly businesses. There are excellent and very exemplary initiatives in this area, including internationally exemplary initiatives in Austria, which are based in particular, Mr Vergestnitsch, on private initiative by companies and their employees. That is to the highest degree to be recognized. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Vergestnichtsch: There is already in Austria!]] Such a comprehensive alliance for families is particularly necessary at a time when partnerships are more crisis-sensitive and the education of children has become more demanding. The number of behavioural children is growing to the extent that the ability and strength to live, family and partnership are decreasing for a variety of reasons. It is therefore important to see the children at the centre, to also ensure that children can be children. That the environment for personality development has changed is a fact. Childhood often means living in a competitive society, in which individual benefits count primarily. Childhood also means being exposed to influences that can cause straining and damaging effects. This is where the media have a very special position and a special responsibility. We are committed to the protection of children and young people, especially in the field of the media. We want to continue to support media education so that children can also be given the responsibility to deal with the media and to make it clear that media cannot be a substitute for children, but that companionship, solidarity, helpfulness must be lived, met, and these values can only be passed on to others. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We are also committed to ensuring that special professional groups, i.e. pedagogical professions, can no longer come into contact with minors with sexual offenders, but that there must also be professional bans in this area, especially in the case of pedagogical professions, especially if there has been an abuse of authority and sexual abuse. This is about the protection of the victims, which is expressly more important to us in this case than the protection of the perpetrators, which is always brought to the meeting as a counter-argument. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Family policy is in particular based on justice between generations. Our society lives in a variety of ways at the expense of our children. We leave our children a state debt inherited from 30 years of previous governments. That is why it is so important to take a step in this area, too, not to make new debts, because debt, public debt greatly restrict future generations' design opportunities, and we assume that all major policy measures in the future need to take account of the impact on future generations, and that we need to think in generation balance sheets and not in annual balance sheets. We have responsibility far beyond our functional periods. We do not want to continue the path of the past and live on pump and at the expense of future generations, but to create a good future for our children.

What the development of democracy is about: Social order is also better taught in the family than in any substitution, whether it be a kid's crib, or a kindergarten, or a school – all this can't replace and should not replace the family. Childcare benefits create new jobs and increase purchasing power in the municipalities and regions considerably! We must consider the amount of 6 000 schilling in relation to income. Considering that the average earnings in my community are between 12,000 and 18,000 Schilling, it is noted that the child care allowance is an increase in family income of up to 50 percent! This is an income that is also spent again. Our study is also scientifically accompanied by the Austrian Institute of Family Research. The results of this work clearly show that at least 70 percent of the money paid remains in the communities! This clearly strengthens the communities financially! Another smile!]] There has been much talk today about the guidance of family policy, and there have been times when ÖVP ministers have openly declared their goals. For example, I remember the former Minister for Family Affairs of ÖVP, Minister Bartenstein, who clearly defined the guiding principle of the ÖVP family policy by saying: The women belong back to the children's room. Even if you find more grueling words today – because you know that women want time for their children, but also time for their profession, and want to build up their own existence – even if You speak differently today, in truth, you do nothing but step by step to implement exactly this family policy objective. We must always look at the environment when it comes to policy, and you are setting up a set of measures to achieve this very objective. One of these measures is the childcare allowance you use – even if you talk about it and don't like to hear it: It is! – extend vocational interruptions and thus simply undermine women's chances of returning to work. However, with a second step, you have made sure that this will also be the case by keeping the termination protection shorter than the time period in which the child care allowance can be obtained. This means that women can be terminated by their employer during the period of child care and will stand without a job at once. Thirdly, in order to keep it safe, you have fully agreed to extend childcare facilities. They are 17 billion schilling – that's what you have to imagine: 17 billion schilling! – in the first three years, i.e. year after year, child care costs 17 billion schilling, but you have not provided a billion schilling per year to minimize the existing needs for childcare facilities in five years. When I talk about the need for childcare facilities, I emphasize: These are really requested places, that concerns communities where women say: We want to work, and we want the best care for our child during this time! [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: Why is there a need at all? Were you not in power for 30 years?]] They also repeatedly call child care money an instrument of poverty reduction, and poverty reduction is something that a rich society like Austria should really take very seriously. – Let us take a look at who you yourself, which groups of persons you, even in the National Action Plan on the reduction of poverty, which has been very poor, rightly define as a threat to poverty! [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: Why is there poverty? Didn't you go to the government?]] For example, these are the families that have more children but in which there is only one income, and of course these are the single parents. What will the situation for these families look like after the three years of child care, some of which will benefit, but others, as we have heard, will receive less money? After these three years, evil awakening comes: After these three years, there is no support anymore. After these three years, you may be off the job, as mentioned earlier, and if you are lucky to have the job, then you will not be able to stand there without childcare facilities and thus at best an income – I am not talking about the single parents at all. The decisive thing, then, is what priorities you set here. If you listen to you today, you become almost sentimental and think: Where are the times when the EIA has yet to speak openly about its social policy objectives in which it has still stated openly where it will set its course? – In the direction that the women will get out and not come back! At that time you have been able to deal seriously with you, now you have to go against any fictitious pictures.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e10248	The next speaker was Mr Schwarzenberger. Voluntary speaking time limit: 6 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e10278	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Mrs. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The education and care of children is an indispensable achievement of the parents for society. A consistent family promotion is therefore an investment in the future of our country. The family is very often represented as the smallest cell in the state, because the family takes over tasks in childcare, but partly also in the care of the elderly. What the small unit can do, this does not have to be delegated to the greater unit, and therefore these families also receive the appropriate support. The expression of this consistent family support is the introduction of child care allowance for all mothers or fathers devoted to childcare. While it has so far not been possible for minor employees, for students, housewives, farmers and self-employed women, to obtain a pension allowance – although in 1990 we introduced a part-time allowance for farmers and women in the industrial sector, for self-employed women, which was only half of the benefit – there is now real justice for all women. Indeed, all mothers or fathers and all children are worth the same, and it does not have to go ahead of self-employed employment with unemployment insurance so that this childcare allowance can be paid out. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] [[President Dr Fassleven takes over the chairmanship.]] The regulations we have now taken also bring about a better compatibility of child education and work performance. It is also possible to adopt part-time employment in order to remain present at the workplace, because the limit on earnings has been increased by four times over the current income limit in the karenz money. However, consistent family support does not end after the third year of the child's life, but continues to be consistent with family support, and that is why there will be an increase in family support for all children by at least 1,200 p.a. from 2003 onwards. I would like to remind you here that in 1998, as part of the tax reform discussion, we introduced a family support under the tax reform in the context of the SPÖ's resistance to the tax reform, which, after all, increased the amount of disposal and family support for a child by 6 000 S in 2000 compared with 1998. These are at least 12.6 billion schillings, which we now pay more for the families per year than we paid in 1998. If there is now another 10 billion schilling in 2002, you can really talk about Austria as the most family-friendly country in the world. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] I still remember very well the statement made by Mrs Prammer, the then Minister of the Family, according to which only working women should receive karenz money. Then, in a large Austrian newspaper, one could find a caricature showing a farmer in the feeding of cattle, holding a small child by the arm, and three children came around her and read in a speech bubble: It's a shame that Mrs. Prammer thinks I've never worked anything! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I would like to say that for the following reason, I do not understand why the SPÖ has shown such resistance to this child care allowance: In the government programme, which is still under negotiation with the SPÖ, we have from 18th to 18th. January 2000, which, while still being decided by the SPÖ Party Bureau, but no longer in the Party Board, has agreed to a pension fund for all: With the conversion of the care allowance into a family benefit from 2002, all mothers or fathers who were not entitled at the time should receive a pension. At the time, an increase in the pension amount to 6 250 S – as it actually takes place – was agreed, of which 250 S should be paid as a pension contribution, so that these times can also be used as pension grounds. In other areas too, the current rules have been substantially agreed with SPÖ. For these reasons, for example, if the SPÖ female trade unionist, who was at least Vice-President of the Austrian Trade Union Confederation, Irmgard Schmidleitner, has declared in a press release that karenz money is a social crime for all mothers. In reality, we are finally creating justice for all children. [[Abg. Dr. Mertel: How was that with the "feary cows"?]] We will therefore continue to shape the framework conditions for our families in such a way that we can continue to be the most family-friendly country in the world in the future.

Ladies and gentlemen! But getting out of here and demanding much more or something else, or even more far-reaching, is enormous if you remember how the child money developed: that the Child Aid Act entered into force at 1.1.1950, and this was made possible by a 3 percent wage ban – an unprecedented solidarity that can only be dreamed of today. This was the foundation for the family compensation fund, which entered into force in 1955 and was provided with 6 percent of the wages. But then the rework has already begun, ladies and gentlemen: With effect from 1.1.1979 and 1981, the income side of the Family Fund was reduced by a total of 25 percent in favour of pension funds. By 1995 this led to 150 billion schilling from the children to the pension funds. It was only 13 billion schilling in 1995! [[Abg. Dr. Mertel: Once you have to see how much has been diverted in 2000 and 2001, how much ÖVP and FPÖ have diverted!]] With 1.5.1995, mine Ladies and gentlemen – you just have to remember – the family allowance was reduced by 1,200 s per year in connection with the first savings package. Huber: Especially last year!]] [[Abg. Only steering measures were two savings packages, which brought incredible loads, did not improve the budget situation and ended up in a disastrous budget deficit. That, ladies and gentlemen of the SPÖ, has brought you away, and you seem to be still proud of that! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] This government has balanced the budget in a year and a half. It has launched a comprehensive administrative reform as well as reforms in schools and universities. It has led to executive improvements, such as the protection of officials in the exercise of their duties and the protection of families when an official dies in the service, or legal assistance in the extremely difficult situation in countless procedures after demonstrations and so on. Now, with the introduction of child benefit, we have shown who it is, for whom the welfare of families is the highest priority. Even more demanding is not art. What you've done, I've just picked you up. I mean, allegorically, when I say, you remind me very much of ash putts: They demand, demand, fight supposedly for families, children, students, teachers. Great Tamtam – and at midnight: Puff, all over, and now just press the shoe. [[Cheerfulness of the Abg. Because there was so much about warmth, affection and love here, and the German Chancellor was actually right in his analysis when he spoke of a colorful world that was experienced with children, I would like to bring you a gallup study that says that children in Austria are treated with unsuspected intelligibility and disapproval. Although 93 percent of all Austrians consider themselves to be tolerant of children, the opposite questions show that this is a lip confession. [[Abg. Lexer is behind the speaker and talks to the Federal Chancellor Dr. Schüssel, who is sitting on the government bank.]] [[Abg. Dr. Mertel: At least the speaker should be kept free!]] In fact, around 60 percent believe that children are not welcome in locals, cinemas, theatres or concerts. Three-quarters of the respondents feel disturbed when children are the same as they are: when they talk loud, eat with their fingers, or want to feel objects. Only one third of the respondents find nothing when children sing on the tram or on the railway. In my opinion, the future must be such that adults are looking to children, treat them fairly, respectfully and lovingly, that all children who live in this country, whether German-speaking or not, can be children and not be accepted only if they adapt to the world of adults that they often cannot grasp or understand at all. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! A slogan of the Austrian children's friends was a long time ago: "More time, more space, more love for our children and for our families". What does that mean? – Fathers and mothers need fair opportunities for a life with children. The worlds of life and life of the people have changed and significantly shape the image of this society. What do families need for a fair chance? – The right to free choice of family form, family-friendly working conditions, a parental partnership model, income security, protection from poverty, quality childcare facilities, job security, clear conditions after separation, uniform standards in the field of youth protection, social assistance and family support, just to mention a few points. Social conditions are the key to a child- and family-friendly Austria. These include security, free access to education, a fair social policy and a human-friendly economic policy, as well as a comprehensive understanding of democracy and respect for human rights. All OECD reports in the past have given an excellent witness to Austrian family policy. We need quality assurance and quality standards in child care in order to produce only one point, especially when private care facilities are required. The impact of the load package is noticeable to people. They often mean uncertainty and are often also a dead end. For example, if Pröll here presents the complaint of a woman as a socialist propaganda, I think that a co-insurance for a woman who has not born children is still true. I mean, Mr Donabauer, it is time to inform the head of state in this regard that you have decided and that this is not propaganda. Ladies and gentlemen! In their programme, the Austrian children's friends call for a society in which all generations, men and women, live together in partnership. Only the solidarity of all can improve the social conditions and thus the living conditions of individuals. We Social Democrats can only meet these demands.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e12422	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The order of the Volksanwälte is regulated in our Constitution: It is up to the three most powerful parties of the National Council to make one member of the People's Procuratorate appointed. This provision in the Constitution, I would like to say here, dates back to 1982. In 1982, as is known, there was a social democratic single government. It is all the more amazing that in some press releases one now reads that it would have been the purpose and purpose of this then determination to record the propor. Rather, it was the case that the sole government decided that each party represented in Parliament should appoint a public prosecutor. So this has nothing to do with the fixing of a proportion, but should also give the opposition parties the opportunity to present a popular attorney. That means: This provision was also, if one follows the will of the legislator of the time, a guarantee for the minority, so that it, no matter what majority is given, has the possibility to order a popular lawyer. It would certainly be appropriate to discuss whether this mode should not be questioned. At the moment, however, we have to order back Volksanwälte. And if you question this mode, you have to, I think, as at the time ensure that the minority parties can also ask a popular lawyer. So if you follow this proposal from the main committee today, you will also take a decision to ensure minority rights. If one were to follow that proposal, which I read in a press release of the Greens, and to press ahead with a hearing, then this hearing would have the effect of making a decision on whether a particular candidate made by a party would be actually elected or not. If this decision or hearing were then taken as a condition, it would be the majority's responsibility to reject a candidate of the minority. It can then be used in the discussion that the party remains the right to be arrested. But if I put these new candidates back into a hearing, it would be possible for the majority parties ad infinitum to exclude candidates for the People's Procuratorate who belong to the minority and the majority are unloving to participate in the Public Procuratorate. That, in turn, would mean that the minority rights guaranteed in this provision are no longer held and the whole ad absurdum is maintained. That means: If you think of such a discussion, you can't make it so easy to say: We are making a hearing, and with that I have done enough to democracy!, but we also need to ensure that the candidate, who has been made famous by the minority, could be rejected by the majority of the People's Prosecutor. I believe that this is the real problem of democracy, which must be resolved. It is important not to close discussions in this direction, but to continue to follow the original idea of the legislator, namely that the appointment of a public prosecutor must also be guaranteed for the minority parties. That is why we will be in favour of this proposal, because we believe that the current regime also represents the minority position and is thus ultimately assured that this minority position will be brought about a breakthrough. Second line of argument: I am firmly convinced that a popular lawyer must be a politician because the politicians ultimately represent the link between the executive organs and the population. Therefore, if one were to bring in only the professional qualification as a lawyer in hearing, then one would not comply with the public prosecutor's political approach, namely to be a mediator between citizens and executives. One should not always pretend that everything that comes from politics is a priority bad, but here the professional politician is asked to act as a mediator. He should also be a member of a political party, as it ensures that the minority positions in this body are also represented. First of all, it can be assumed that the present proposal is a political one that we all know. One can have more sympathy with one another than with the other, but in the end it is a proposal that takes into account on the one hand the minority position, on the other hand, but on the other hand creates the prerequisite for political positioning, namely the possibility of giving the People's Procuratorate a political mandate, namely to be a mediator between citizens and executives. The people who are elected have a certain degree of awareness in this House, therefore, as I believe, there is nothing against an agreement to this proposal, although a discussion on ordermaking would be quite fruitful in the future, an order mode with the certainty that minority positions, as they are currently constitutional, will continue to be maintained.

Ladies and gentlemen! § 209 was created in the mid-1970s, in the conviction that the so-called embossing theory is reality, that one can be seduced into homosexuality and that it is therefore necessary to provide for appropriate protection for young men male sex. In the meantime, however, the doubts already expressed in the decision-making process of this law have been fully resolved. ParlaMint-AT_2001-06-07-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00072_d7e15682	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I think it is very welcome that we are today unanimously amending the Minority School Act for Carinthia – a law that the Constitutional Court has redefined for us. This means that from autumn 2001 in the bilingual schools in Carinthia must also be taught in the fourth classes of Slovenian or, in other words, that children have the opportunity to deepen their knowledge in the second national language. The gift of bilingualism, which children in the bilingual area are somehow placed in the cradle, is a great opportunity for them, but also for the country. It is not only about the fact that a second language makes learning a third much easier or that the sense of speech and getting to know another culture also bring with it the understanding and tolerance of another group. I think the whole thing also has an economic aspect. We are facing EU enlargement, and Slovenia is an important trading partner for Austria. The importance of this partner will surely increase with EU accession. The opportunities offered by multilingualism therefore need to be strengthened and promoted, whether in the public schools or in kindergartens. Because there the basis is laid for a truly living language with all its facets and intermediate tones. One thing, however, is thoughtful: We are signing the European Charter of Regional or Minority Languages, we are now deciding on bilingual teaching in the fourth classes in Carinthia, and yet there is still a flow in this country unnoticed or ignored by many, which can be called retroactive. In fact, suddenly, management posts at bilingual schools in Carinthia are no longer occupied with bilingual persons, although this has been the case since 1945 and has worked very well. That is to say, there are now directors of bilingual schools who are not powerful in this second national language. [[Abg. Dr. Mertel: Under Haider!]] That is a contradiction in itself. The National Law on Services provides that teachers in bilingual schools must be qualified for both languages. Directors must represent Members who are absent in case of need. However, they must also be a liaison point between schools and the environment of the school. The question now is how this can work well if they are not powerful in this second national language. From this point of view, I do not understand why you, Mrs Minister, are simply not interested in this issue or do it again and again as a matter of country. It cannot be that we must, in turn, try the courts or that the courts must make an effort to reach a decision. It would be no easier than to add a pass to the law, namely a federal law, which states that bilingual schools should be occupied in a bilingual environment with bilingual children and bilingual teachers with bilingual directors. Here you could simply save time and nerves by applying this law, Mrs. Minister. Alone, it seems to me that there is no political will. I think with the tactics of ignoring, you also ignore the European Year of Languages, which you often verbally support. I believe that this year of European languages, in which you wanted to focus on the neighbouring languages, would require clear words on the situation in Carinthia. Thank you.

It is always described as having no problem, they can do anything in Austria anyway. Body and sensory disabilities have the possibility to be integrated from gymnasium to university. The fact is: Unfortunately, it is not. The fact is: It has already been changed – unfortunately, I have to say – a few years ago, even in times of the old government, § 8(3) of the school compulsory law, which has provided that there is explicit support for physically disabled persons in the field of secondary school, not only in the folk school, a special educational need. We have received a whole lattice of letters, in which those affected complain that there are no more support measures for their visually impaired children. You didn't get what they used to get in extra hours due to the savings. This has happened, and we have therefore also proposed that this be reintroduced in the field of secondary education. This is the request that is being made today, but unfortunately rejected by the government parties. In this area, I would really like to see how the situation is with people with disabilities. There are examples of blind children. Or all the spatial problems: I know a lot of schools where it would be impossible for the class to be attended. There are many gymnasias where it is not even possible for a wheelchair driver to come to the classes. That's why you can't say it's all there. There may be no need for support in itself, but a package of measures would really be needed to achieve integration in order to open up the possibility. That's what we want. Let us look at this in the form of a parliamentary survey! Let's see where the problems lie and try to find really adequate solutions!
